欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い（図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい（図表5）。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。しかし、当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （ Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる（図表7）。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している（図表8）。ユーロ安は、輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

Document 038: 2026-11-12_［みずほ経済インサイト］アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大_111294bf

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-12_［みずほ経済インサイト］アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大_111294bf.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

1

アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
供給制約への対応力が投資の持続性を左右

○ アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。その背景には、AI・クラウド需要の拡大や各国・
地域の投資誘致策、クラウド事業者の戦略、建設コストの低さなどが挙げられる 
○ データセンター投資が増加することは、総固定資本形成の増加や、半導体・サーバーなどの輸出
拡大、雇用創出、技術・知識の移転につながる可能性がある 
○ 今後も需要拡大が見込まれる一方、立地地域が抱える電力・水などのインフラ負荷は大きい。 
投資の経済効果と負荷とのバランスを踏まえた政策対応が、投資拡大の持続性を左右するだろう 
１．アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。図表1は、同地域におけるデータセンター関連の対内
直接投資計画額を示したものである。データセンター投資は2024年頃から大きく増加し、2026年上半
期も高水準を維持している。主要な投資先であるASEAN向け投資が一段と拡大したほか、2025年以降は
インド、オーストラリアでも投資が急増している。 
また、アジアのFDI流入におけるデータセンターの存在感も高まっている。同地域の対内直接投資計
画額全体に占めるデータセンターの割合は、2025年に約30％まで上昇した（図表2）。 
図表 1 アジアのデータセンター直接投資計画額  図表 2 FDI 流入額中のデータセンター投資比率

（注）fDi Marketsが集計した直接投資額。投資額は企業の計画
発表内容による。投資額が非公表の場合、fDi Marketsに
よる推計値を含む。ASEAN：インドネシア、マレーシア、

２．アジアにおいてデータセンター投資がなぜ増加しているのか 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している背景には、投資受入国におけるデジタル関連需要の増
加や、それを受けた投資誘致の活発化、また投資側企業にとってコスト面などから当該地域の相対的
な優位性が増していることが挙げられる。 
実際、投資受入国ではデジタルサービスに対する需要が拡大している。Gelvanovska-Garcia et al.
（2024）によれば、東アジア・太平洋地域では、インターネットの普及や経済のデジタル化を背景に、
製造業や医療など幅広い分野でクラウドサービスの導入が進んでいると指摘している。こうしたサー
ビスの利用拡大は、データの処理・保存を担うデータセンターの需要を高めることにつながる。 
また、各国政府がデータセンター誘致を活発化させていることも、アジアにおけるデータセンター
投資を後押ししている。図表3は、各国のデータセンター関連の優遇策を示したものだが、各国で誘致
を目的とした優遇政策がとられている。タイやインドでは、一定の条件を満たすデータセンター事業
や関連事業者に対して、所得税等の免税措置にまで踏み込んでいる。 
対して、投資側企業にとっては、需要地の近くにデータセンターを整備することによる、自社サー
ビスの品質向上が期待される1。事実、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件の企業発表にお
いても、アジアへの投資目的として、現地企業や政府への低遅延サービス提供、通信環境の強靭化な
どが挙げられている2。 
サービス品質向上に加え、ASEANやインド等における建設コストの低さも投資を後押しする要因とな
っている。図表4は、データセンターにおけるIT負荷容量1メガワットあたりの建設コストを比較した
ものだが、インドやベトナム、インドネシアなどはデータセンターの建設費用が相対的に小さく、シ
ンガポール等の地域に比べ、低コストでデータセンターの容量増強が可能である。

３．データセンター投資はアジア経済に何をもたらすのか 
データセンター投資の拡大は、アジア経済にどのような影響をもたらすのだろうか。主なポイント
は以下の4つである。 
まず第一に、総固定資本形成の押し上げである。データセンターの設置時、企業は建物を建設する
ほか、サーバーや受変電、冷却、通信等の設備を購入する。こうした企業活動は、GDP統計上では総固
定資本形成中の建設・機械設備投資として計上され、経済成長率の押し上げ要因となる3。実際、オー
ストラリアでは2025年以降、情報・通信部門の機械投資がGDP成長率を押し上げており、データセンタ
ー投資拡大の影響がうかがえる。 
また第二のポイントは、AI関連財の輸出拡大である。東アジアやASEANは、半導体やサーバー等の製
造拠点であり、製品需要の拡大は輸出の追い風となる。図表5に示す通り、アジアのAI関連財輸出はAI
ブームのもとで急拡大が続いている。とりわけ、2026年以降は中国などアジア域内向け輸出の増勢が
顕著であり、アジア域内におけるデータセンター設置拡大の影響がみてとれる。 
そして第三のポイントは、雇用の拡大である。データセンターの設置時に建設や設備設置の作業員
が必要となるほか、稼働後も設備管理やサーバー運用に関わる人材の雇用が創出される可能性がある。 
最後は、技術・知識の波及である。データセンターを運営する外国企業が現地企業と取引すること
により、外国企業から現地企業へ技術が伝わることが期待される。例えば、電力・冷却・通信設備の
設計や施工、保守管理にかかるノウハウを、現地企業が獲得するケースなどがある。 
ただし、経済波及効果の実現には不確実性が伴う点は留意する必要がある。中でも、雇用への波及
効果は限定的なものに留まる可能性がある。というのも、fDi Marketsが集計する投資計画データによ
ると（図表6）、海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの直接雇用創出数は、データセンター投資の場合、全
産業平均よりも少数にとどまるからだ。

４．データセンター投資の先行きとリスク要因 
今後のデータセンター投資は、需要面での拡大余地が大きいと見込まれる一方、供給面では様々な
制約が表面化する可能性がある。 
まず、需要面については、クラウド需要の拡大がデータセンターの投資を促す公算が大きい。実際、
主要な民間調査会社の予想によれば、電子商取引やAI利活用の浸透によりデータの保存・処理需要が
増加し、アジアにおけるクラウド市場の拡大が続くとされている4。 
一方、先行きの供給制約となりうる要因も少なくない。具体的には、電力・水などの資源制約と、
政策変更の2つである。 
資源制約については、第一に電力供給の制約がある。アジアでは、今後も電力需要の拡大が見込ま
れる一方で、それに見合った電力供給の拡大が容易ではないからだ。図表7は、IEAが2026年2月に公表
した、2026～2030年の年平均増加率の予測を示している。掲載した各国・地域ではいずれも電力需要
の増加が見込まれ、特にインドやベトナムなどで高い伸びとなっていることがわかる。産業活動の拡
大や冷房需要の増加、データセンターの集積などが需要拡大をけん引する格好だ。 
一方、電力供給の伸びは、需要と比べ、緩やかなものにとどまる可能性が高い。IEAによれば、東南
アジアの電力生産量は2026～2030年に年平均5.2％の増加と、電力需要の伸び（年平均5.4％）を下回
り、電力需給のひっ迫が続くことが想定されている5。 
また、データセンターの立地が特定地域に集中しやすい点にも留意が必要だ6。特定地域で電力需給
がひっ迫した場合、送電網の不足などで他地域からの融通ができない場合がある7。 
資源制約については、電力のみならず、水の安定供給という課題もある。冷却に大量の水を使用す
るデータセンターでは、施設の新設・拡張に伴って水需要が増加すると見込まれるからだ。必要な水
量を確保できなければ、施設の稼働が制約されることになりかねない。ASEAN事務局（2025）は、デー
タセンターの冷却に伴う水需要の増加は、農業・工業・住民生活向けの水利用と競合する可能性があ
ると指摘している8。 
二点目は、政策変更のリスクである。雇用拡大等
を通じた地場経済への貢献度の低さや、資源利用や
安全性をめぐる地域住民からの反発などを背景に、
投資許認可の厳格化や新規開発の制限が行われる
可能性がある。 
地場経済への貢献については、先述の通り、デー
タセンターの直接的な雇用創出効果は他の投資に
比べて限られる。その結果、受入国・地域が期待し
ていた経済効果よりも現地経済への貢献が小さい
となった場合、投資誘致や優遇税制の見直しにつな
がる可能性は否定できない。また、地域住民からの
反発については、図表8で示す通り、既にマレーシ
ア、インドネシア、インド、オーストラリアなどで、
水利用や生活環境への影響などを巡り、データセン

ター投資への抗議や反対運動が起きている。 
こうした状況のもと、一部の国ではすでにデータセンター投資関連の規制を見直す動きが広がって
いる。実際にタイでは、政府による委員会組織（データセンタービジネス委員会）が、データセンタ
ー166件の建設や許可手続きを一時停止した。停止の理由としては、資源の利用、立地、安全性に関す
る全国的な基準の策定が必要であることが挙げられている9。タイ政府は投資誘致を維持すると示して
いるものの、予見可能性の低下から、進捗中案件の見直しや計画中案件の中止につながるリスクは完
全に拭えない。

５．供給制約への対応力がデータセンター投資の行方を左右 
アジアでは、AI・クラウド需要の拡大を背景に、今後もデータセンター投資の増加が続くと見込ま
れる。ただし、巨額の投資に比べて雇用創出効果は限定的とみられる一方、電力・水などのインフラ
負荷は大きく、投資の拡大が必ずしも受入国・地域に十分な経済的便益をもたらすとは限らない。 
こうした中、今後のデータセンター投資では、建設コストの低さや税制優遇に加え、電力・水の安
定供給や規制・許認可の予見可能性などが、投資先を選別する上で一段と重要になると考えられる。
供給制約が深刻化する地域では、投資計画の遅延や縮小が生じる一方、安定的な事業環境を確保でき
る地域に投資が集中する可能性もある。 
受入国にとっても、単に投資額の拡大を追求するのではなく、国内産業への波及やインフラ整備の
負担を見極めることが重要となる。今後のアジアにおけるデータセンター投資は、需要拡大が成長を
支える一方で、供給制約への対応力の違いが、各国・地域のへの投資配分を左右することになるだろ
う。

図表 8 データセンターを巡る抗議活動の例

延が減少し、データ送受信にかかる時間が短期化すると指摘されている。 
2 fDi Markets や生成 AI による情報収集により、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件に関する企業発表の内容を確認

付加価値の増加につながるわけではない。

の。

電網容量を大幅に増強する必要が生じる可能性があること、また電力供給が途切れないよう安定的な電力網を構築する必要があ
ることを指摘している。 
7 ベトナム北部では、2023 年 5～6 月に断続的な停電が発生し、多くの家庭や企業に影響が広がった。World Bank（2023）
“Taking Stock, August 2023: Making Public Investment Work for Growth”は、送電網整備の遅れにより、南部の余剰発電

8 ASEAN（2025）“ASEAN Guide for Sustainable Data Centre Development”によれば、データセンターの中でもハイパースケ
ール施設の冷却システムでは、水使用量が 1 日当たり最大 150 万リットルに達する場合があり、干ばつが発生しやすい地域の水
供給や生態系に大きな負荷を与えうると説明している。また、水利用の効率化を促す政策的枠組みがなければ、データセンター
の水需要が、農業・工業・地域住民に必要な水の確保を圧迫する可能性を指摘している。

7～9 月期実質GDP は年率▲0.1％と 
ほぼ横ばいの結果（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの結果。
4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服 
○ 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率
を下押し。一方、設備投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）は、ほぼ横ばい 
内閣府が本日公表した2026年7～9月期の「四半期別GDP速報（QE）」（ 1次
速報値）では、実質GDPが前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの
結果になった。4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.7％）から減速し、経済
の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服した格好だ（図表）。 
需要項目別にみると、内需の柱である個人消費の減少や、輸出の小幅減少
が成長率の下押し要因になった。一方、設備投資は堅調に拡大し、成長率を
押し上げた。その他では、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイ
ナス要因になったが、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要
因として現れ、両者の成長率に対する影響は概ね打ち消し合っている。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
図表 2026 年 7～9 月期 GDP（1 次速報）

2

個人消費は2四半期連続
で減少。根強い節約志向
や、耐久財の特需剥落が
影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と、2四半期連続で減少した（4～6月期は同＋
0.0％から同▲0.3％に下方修正）。足元の消費の弱さの背景には、複合的な
要因があると考えられる。 
品目別の内訳をみると、衣料品などの半耐久財が前期比▲1.4％（4～6月
期：同＋0.4％）と、大幅に減少した。長雨などの天候不順の影響で、衣料
品の消費が弱含んだとみられる。 
食品・エネルギー・各種消耗品などの非耐久財は、前期比▲0.2％（ 4～6月
期：同▲1.9％ ）と 2四半期連続で減少した。物価高を背景とする根強い節約
志向が影響したようだ。今回のインフレ局面が本格化する前の2020年と比較
すると、必需品が多い非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6年半でおよそ1.3
倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上昇が、消費者の体
感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、購入数量抑制につながっている可
能性がある。 
また、耐久財が前期比▲3.5％（4～6月期：同＋3.6％）と大幅減に転じた
ことも、7～9月期の消費を下押しした。これは、自動車とエアコンの「特需」
剥落が原因だ。自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に
廃止されたことを受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていた
が、8月以降は再び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年
4月の省エネ基準引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要
が生じていたが、景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入
った後は一服した模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減
少もエアコンなど季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
サービス消費は前期比＋0.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.1％）とプラスに転じた
が、伸びは緩慢だ。イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、サービスや価格帯、顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
一方、設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と、3四半期ぶりに前期比プラスへ転じた。
4～6月期も同▲0.9％から同▲0.4％と、依然マイナスながらも上方修正され
た。 
設備投資の関連指標では、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財総供給指
数（除く輸送機械）や、第3次産業活動指数におけるソフトウェア業の実質
売上高が改善している。世界的なAIブームを背景とするデータセンター等の
AI関連投資や、人手不足を受けたソフトウェア導入などの省力化投資が影響
したとみられる。 
ただし、7～9月期の変化率には、前期発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特

### Page 3

3

許権の海外譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少）の反動も影響している。足
元の設備投資の伸びは実態よりもやや強い結果とみられ、多少割り引いて評
価する必要があろう。 
住宅投資は前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.6％ ）と 、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスになった。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～9月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上
昇による需要低迷に加えて、地震や台風などによる着工・進捗の遅れが発生
した可能性がある。 
民間在庫は成長率へのプ
ラス寄与が縮小 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、±0.0％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラス幅が縮小した。4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不
足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油
の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響が
一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラス 
公的需要は前期比＋1.8％（4～6月期：同▲0.9％）と、2四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスとなった。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じたことだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変動の寄与度
が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備蓄の減少が
小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公的在庫の減
少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.5％Ptとプラス寄与した。 
一方、その他の公的需要項目では、公共投資が前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：
同▲0.6％）と2四半期連続のマイナスになった。台風等により土木関連工事
の進捗に遅れが発生した可能性がある。また、政府消費は前期比▲0.0％（ 4
～6月期：同＋1.5％）とほぼ横ばいにとどまった。4～6月期は高校授業料・
小学校給食費の無償化開始により高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした
特殊要因が剥落した。 
原油代替調達で輸入が大
幅に増加し、外需はマイ
ナス寄与に反転 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.2％（4～6月期：同＋1.6％）と小幅に減少し
た一方、輸入が同＋2.5％（4～6月期：同▲1.3％）と大幅に増加した。その
結果、実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.6％Pt（4～6月期：同＋
0.6％Pt）と、マイナスに反転した。 
輸入の大幅な増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化に
より4～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホ
ルムズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの
輸入量が確保されている。 
こうした輸入の大幅増加は、7～9月期のGDP成長率に対し▲0.5％Ptのマイ
ナス寄与になった。もっとも、先述したように代替調達の進展に伴って国家
備蓄原油の放出が抑制され、公的在庫変動が＋0.5％Ptのプラス寄与になっ

### Page 4

4

ており、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果は公的在庫変動によりほぼ相殺さ
れた。 
輸出は、米国関税影響で落ち込んだ2025年7～9月期以来、4四半期ぶりの
前期比マイナスになった。内訳をみると、サービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（ 4
～6月期：同＋8.9％ ）と 急減したことが主因だ。前期の4～6月期に特許権の
海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げたが、7～9月期はその反動減が現れたた
めであり（これは先述した7～9月期の設備投資の押し上げ要因でもある）、
一時的な動きと言えよう。なお、インバウンド需要（非居住者家計の国内で
の直接購入）は、前期比▲2.4％（4～6月期＋1.6％）と減少した。日中対立
の継続による中国人訪日客の少なさや、航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の
伸び悩みが影響したとみられる。 
一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（ 4～6月期：同▲0.8％）と堅調に回復した。
旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体・電子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸
出がけん引したようだ。今後はサービス輸出の特殊要因が剥落するなか、財
輸出の拡大傾向が続き、輸出は全体として増加基調をたどると考えられる。 
2026 年度後半の実質GDP
は小幅プラスと予想。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期のプラス成長から減速し、ほぼ
横ばいの結果になった。先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～
2027年3月）の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。
個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろ
う。2026年度全体の実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平
均水準が押し上げられることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％
弱とまずまずの成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、コア消費者物価指数（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前年比＋2％台後半まで
上昇すると予想される（電気・ガス料金支援の再開と、ガソリン補助金縮小
を考慮）。また、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを中心
とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、一時的に消費の抑制要因になろ
う。ただし、2026年12月～2027年2月に政府の電気・ガス料金支援が実施さ
れることで、2027年1～3月期を中心に消費が下支えされる見込みだ。 
年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調査）では、
2026年度の設備投資計画（ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投資額（除く
土地投資額））が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した（6月調査：同＋8.8％ ）。
生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、設備の不足感が
強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、設備投資は今後拡大傾向で推移して
いく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」（特定生産性向上

### Page 5

5

設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、また、投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。 
日銀の10月会合は、物価
目標到達を確認した歴史
的な日に。今後は、12月会
合で追加利上げを行う見
込み 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。事前に報じられていたように、今回会合で
日銀は「消費者物価の基調的な上昇率は2％の「物価安定の目標」と概ね整
合的な水準」との見解を示した。2％の「物価安定の目標」を定めた2013年
1月から数えて、13年9カ月でようやく物価目標に到達した格好だ。これまで
長きにわたり実施してきたデフレ脱却のための特別な金融政策が、ついに終
わりを迎えたという意味で、歴史的な日になったと言える。 
今後は、基調的なインフレ率を2％程度の水準で「定着」させるために、物
価上振れ・下振れのリスクを都度評価して金融政策を調整していくことにな
る。当面は、中東情勢、AI関連需要、為替相場の変動（円安圧力）などを背
景に物価上振れリスクが強い状況が続くと見込まれ、日銀は金融環境への影
響を確認しながら利上げを継続するだろう。 
次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％）は、次回12月17・18日会合と予想する。前
回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて「3カ月に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、
日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の「半年強に1回」から加速していることを印
象付けるものになろう。最終的には、2027年半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％
まで引き上げていくとみている。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

NVIDIA Corp. (NVDA-US)  
財報 FIRST TAKE — FY3Q27 NVDA 營收與財測全面優於預期，且上修 FY2028 營收成長預期

個股基本資料：

 評論及分析 (Overall Summary) 
NVDA FY3Q27 營收、獲利及 Q4 財測都優於市場預期。財報公布後，股價
盤後一度上漲約 4%；董事會主席在法說會上表明不讓黃仁勳回任，漲幅一
度收斂。隨後暫代執行長 Kress 將 FY2028 營收成長指引由~70% 上修至
~75%，盤後漲幅再度擴大近 5%。一掃市場原先對公司可能會撤回長期展
望的擔憂；加上 Vera Rubin 創下公司史上最快放量，世代轉換十分順利。 
展望後市，市場普遍認為，AI 算力需求仍受供給限制，看好公司 FY2028 營
運能見度。CPU、網通與每座資料中心的產品含量提升，有助抵銷 ASIC 市
占提升的壓力；以 CY2027 本益比衡量，NVDA 評價仍低於費半。惟後續變
數在於 CEO 接班、美國新出口管制，以及相關調查與訴訟風險。整體而
言，基本面動能延續，惟治理與政策風險是評價折價的主因。 
公司財報重點數據： 
NVDA FY3Q27 營收 1,158 億美元，QoQ+20%、YoY+103%，優於市場預
期的 1,090 億美元。Non-GAAP 毛利率 74.9%，優於市場預期，主因產品組
合較佳。Non-GAAP EPS 2.68 美元，YoY+111%，優於市場預期的 2.47 美
元；自由現金流 437.8 億美元，較上季的 213.4 億美元明顯回升。本季以庫
藏股與股利合計返還股東 281 億美元（庫藏股約 220 億美元、現金股利約 
60 億美元），創歷史新高。董事會於 9 月核准增加 1,500 億美元庫藏股額
度，季末剩餘額度約 2,273 億美元，計畫於 FY2028 年底前執行完畢。 
業務部門表現： 
資料中心：營收 1,076 億美元，QoQ+21%、YoY+110%，優於市場預期。
其中 Vera Rubin 首季營收近 200 億美元，網通營收逾 210 億美元。 
⚫ 超大型雲端（Hyperscale ） 營收 600 億 美 元 ， QoQ+23% 、
YoY+71%，受惠 Vera Rubin 放量及 Grace Blackwell 需求延續。雲端
產業積壓訂單已由三個月前的 2 兆美元增至 2.5 兆美元。 
⚫ ACI&E（AI 雲端、工業、企業）營收 470 億美元，QoQ+18%、YoY
近 3 倍 。 季 增 動 能 來 自  Vera Rubin 出貨給主要新興 雲 端 業者
（Neocloud）及前沿模型實驗室自建資料中心。主權 AI 營收 YoY 逾 
3 倍；金融業地端營收過去 12 個月接近 50 億美元，年增逾 1 倍。 
⚫ 本季對中國出貨的授權 Hopper 資料中心產品營收為 24 億美元。 
邊緣運算：營收 82 億美元，QoQ+14%、YoY+42%，優於市場預期，主要
由企業端 Blackwell 工作站及實體 AI 邊緣運算需求帶動。

公司展望：
(1).預估 FY4Q27 營收 1,300 億美元（±3%），優於市場預期；本季多一
週，且財測未納入任何中國資料中心運算營收。(2).預估 FY4Q27 毛利率
71%~72%，大致符合市場預期。公司表示 4Q 為毛利率低點，因需履行舊
價格訂單，新價格要到新年度才實施；FY2028 預計回升至 72%~73%，與
上季說法一致。(3). 預估 FY4Q27 GAAP / Non-GAAP 營業費用約 110 億 /
107 億 美 元 ， 反 映 多 出 一 週 及 交 接 相 關 成 本 ； FY2027 稅 率 維 持
16%~18%。(4). FY2028 全年營收成長由約 70%上修至約 75%（考量需求
成長、供應鏈支援增加，以及客戶資料中心整備與資金取得情況後的結
果）。(5). 預期 FY2028 CPU 營收、企業營收都將倍增以上。Vera CPU 新
增 CoreWeave、Meta、Nscale、OpenAI 等客戶。 (6). 產品藍圖不變：
Vera Rubin Ultra 預計明年推出，Feynman 預計 2028 年推出。DSX POD

模組化 AI 工廠預計明年初開始出貨美國及中歐客戶。(7). 供應鏈：本季新增
與 載 板 及 先 進 封 裝 業 者 的 產 能 合 作 ， 採 購 承 諾 增 至  3,900 億 美 元 。
Neocloud 相關承諾 320 億美元，公司表示將嚴格評估後續投資、承諾與保
證。(8). 中國：已停止出貨授權 Hopper 資料中心產品。美國商務部本週公
布新出口管制規則，公司仍在評估，但表示需求遠大於供給，預期營運仍維
持強勁。
電話會議 Q&A 重點摘要：
(1). FY2028 營收增速上修依據：公司表示，上修反映需求持續成長及新增
的供給；新出口管制仍在評估，但對 75% 有信心。(2). CEO 交接：董事會
主席開場即表明不會讓黃仁勳回任，並已委託獵才公司啟動遴選，內外部人
選都會考慮，且不會倉促決定。公司強調「營運一切如常」，客戶與供應商
全力支持，記憶體與先進封裝產能皆已確保。(3). Neocloud 融資與保證條
款： 公司表示，保證需待客戶完成融資、資料中心可啟用後才生效，這只

是說明現行做法，並非條款變更；未來支持客戶將更具選擇性，資本配置以
庫藏股為優先。(4). 2028 年放緩疑慮與產能分配：針對美國政局變化可能
帶來的政策風險，公司表示 Agentic AI 需求將延續到 2028 年，並持續與政
府溝通。產能分配依客戶資料中心整備度、資金到位及承接能力而定，所有
客戶價格一致。
公司簡介、競爭對手與公司自身價值：
NVIDIA 已轉型為 AI 基礎設施平台業者，護城河建立在 GPU、Vera CPU、
NVLink／Spectrum-X 網通、CPO 共同封裝光學與 CUDA 軟體生態系的
「極端共同設計」。公司表示，Vera Rubin 的每瓦 token 產出為 GB300 的
10 倍 ， 客 戶 導 入 時 間 只 有 Grace Blackwell 的 一 半 。 公 司 並 宣 布 收 購
Hugging Face，把影響力延伸至開源模型分發層。面對雲端業者加速自研
ASIC，NVDA 憑藉全端平台、供應鏈產能布局及生態系融資機制，維持 AI

算力市場的主導地位。

Better Interest Spread — Group interest spread widened 8bps qoq to 6.95%, 
mainly driven by better China spread. China spread improved 19bps qoq, as loan 
yield increased 8bps qoq and funding cost was 11bps lower qoq. Taiwan loan spread 
narrowed 7bps qoq, primarily due to 6bps qoq higher funding cost amid Fed rate 
hike and tightening domestic liquidity. 
Muted Loan Growth — Group loans grew by 1% qoq/4% yoy, mainly driven by 
ASEAN portfolio growth and RMB appreciation. Taiwan portfolio was flat qoq/-1% 
yoy. China portfolio was +2% qoq/flat yoy in local currency (+3% qoq/+11% yoy in 
NTD). Management suggested that the China business has achieved positive loan 
growth in the past three months and they expected positive momentum ahead. 
ASEAN portfolio was+2% qoq/+10% yoy,  
Broadly Stable China Asset Quality, Higher ASEAN Credit Cost — Group 
delinquency ratio slightly increased 3bps qoq to 5.16%. Taiwan delinquency ratio 
increased 20bps qoq, while NPL formation improved qoq. China delinquency ratio 
was stable qoq, but NPL formation slightly picked up qoq per our estimates. Group 
credit cost was 2.49%, +11bps qoq, mainly due to higher ASEAN credit costs on the 
back of macro uncertainties especially in Cambodia. 
Implications — Despite steady profit growth in 10M26 and stabilizing China asset 
quality, Chailease shares continued to de-rate this year amid concerns over growth. 
Management sounded more upbeat on China loan growth outlook at the results 
briefing, and the impact from auto financing business on Taiwan loan growth should 
gradually fade. FY26E dividend yield of 6% is attractive, in our view.

Chailease Holding (5871.TW)

2
Chailease Holding
Valuation
Our target price for Chailease of NT$130 is based on 1.2x 2026E P/B, using a Gordon Growth model. Our valuation
methodology takes into account a sustainable ROE estimate of 12.1%, a cost-of-equity assumption of 11.1%, and a long-term
growth rate of 6.0%. We use a blended approach to calculate both risk-free rates and long-term growth rates taking into
account local estimates on these parameters in the major markets in which Chailease operates, namely Taiwan, China, and

Thailand, and weighted them by asset mix.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the Chailease stock from reaching our target price are: [1] slower-than-expected asset
growth momentum; [2] asset quality deterioration in different markets; [3] higher operating expenses, which drag bottom-line
growth; [4] earlier-than-expected capital-raising that dilutes ROE and BVPS; and [5] ASEAN expansion that is slow in
contributing to group earnings. Key upsides risks to our target price include: [1] faster-than-expected asset quality recovery as

macro uncertainties ease; [2] higher-than-expected asset growth momentum.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

附錄一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上 
中立 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10% 
降低持股 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於(-10%)以下 
未評等 沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

6개월 목표주가 2,000,000

현재주가 
(26.10.21) 
1,417,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

자료: 유안타증권

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

Investment Implications
We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and
broaden portfolio exposure. As
earnings growth moderates and
market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.

Investment Implications
With China’s growth model still favoring
exports over domestic consumption,
resource protectionism and an
emphasis on domestic investment not
just in the U.S. but around the globe are

likely to continue to be themes in 2027.
We remain overweight the U.S., as its
well-diversified services-led economy
should be relatively less exposed to
China’s manufactured goods exports.
Still, the impact of competition from
China on U.S. technology supremacy is
an ongoing key risk to watch.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Chicago Fed National Activity Index is a monthly index designed to gauge overall economic activity and related inflationary pressure.
Consumer Price Index is a measure of the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services. Indexes are available for the
U.S. and various geographic areas.
National Federation of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index is a monthly composite economic indicator that measures the health, confidence, and expectations of small

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

US and Asia ex-Japan would continue to lead in

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 363: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-19).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.
